## 2. Full Semantic Chunks by Document

### 1. On Computable Numbers, with an Application to the Entscheidungsproblem (`paper-on-computable-numbers-with-an-application-to-the-ent`)

- **Metadata:** `academic_paper` | Status: `to-read` | Priority: `high` | Published: `1936` | Authors: Turing
- **Navigation:** [Inspect on Dashboard](index.html#doc=paper-on-computable-numbers-with-an-application-to-the-ent) | [External Source](https://www.cs.virginia.edu/~robins/Turing_Paper_1936.pdf)

#### `paper-on-computable-numbers-with-an-application-to-the-ent#chunk-01` — Why It Matters & Core Contribution (31 words)

**Universal computation** - defines the Turing machine and the boundary between computable and uncomputable; the premise that a machine can in principle compute anything a brain computes underlies the entire field

- **Matched Concepts:** none

### 2. A Logical Calculus of the Ideas Immanent in Nervous Activity (`paper-a-logical-calculus-of-the-ideas-immanent-in-nervous`)

#### `paper-a-logical-calculus-of-the-ideas-immanent-in-nervous#chunk-01` — Why It Matters & Core Contribution (39 words)

### 30. Curriculum Area Guide: 🏺 Classics: Historical Roots of Neural Networks (29 Papers) (`curriculum-area-classics`)

- **Metadata:** `note` | Status: `read` | Priority: `high` | Published: `2026-11` | Authors: kitsumiko/papers Contributors
- **Navigation:** [Inspect on Dashboard](index.html#doc=curriculum-area-classics) | [External Source](https://github.com/kitsumiko/papers/blob/main/learning/classics.md)

#### `curriculum-area-classics#chunk-01` — Curriculum Area Overview: Classics: Historical Roots of Neural Networks (118 words)

**Overview**: This area tells the story of how the field arrived at trainable feedforward networks — the seventy-year lineage behind everything else in this collection. You'll follow the thread from Turing's universal machines and the McCulloch-Pitts formal neuron, through Rosenblatt's perceptron and the first AI winter, to the quiet independent inventions of [backpropagation](https://github.com/kitsumiko/papers/blob/main/learning/glossary.md#backpropagation) and [stochastic gradient descent](https://github.com/kitsumiko/papers/blob/main/learning/glossary.md#sgd-stochastic-gradient-descent), the associative memories of Amari and Hopfield, the connectionist revival of the 1980s, and the [vanishing gradient](https://github.com/kitsumiko/papers/blob/main/learning/glossary.md#vanishing-gradient-problem) wall whose eventual circumvention in 2006 launched modern [deep learning](https://github.com/kitsumiko/papers/blob/main/learning/glossary.md#deep-learning). These papers are history rather than prerequisites — but reading them explains *why* [CNNs](https://github.com/kitsumiko/papers/blob/main/learning/glossary.md#cnn-convolutional-neural-network), [RNNs](https://github.com/kitsumiko/papers/blob/main/learning/glossary.md#rnn-recurrent-neural-network), and gradient training look the way they do, and how often the field forgot and reinvented its own ideas.

- **Matched Concepts:** `concept:dnn`
- **Technology Areas:** AI & Machine Learning, Deep Learning Architectures, Semiconductor Engineering
- **Taxonomy Hierarchy:** `Artificial Intelligence & Machine Learning`; `Artificial Intelligence & Machine Learning > Deep Neural Networks`; `Hardware & Semiconductor Systems`

#### `curriculum-area-classics#chunk-02` — Subtopics & Pedagogical Structure (51 words)

This area comprises **29 curated papers and documents** organized across 7 subtopics: Computation & the First Neurons, Perceptrons & the First Winter, Backprop's Prehistory & Associative Memory, Attractors & Self-Organization, The Connectionist Revival, Limits, Winter, and the Way Out, Guides & Retrospectives. Domain focus: artificial intelligence neural networks deep learning architectures.

- **Matched Concepts:** none
- **Technology Areas:** AI & Machine Learning, Automated Reasoning & Mathematical Logic, Electronic Design Automation, Graph Analysis & Combinatorial Optimization, Memory Subsystems & Chiplets, SAT & SMT Decision Procedures, Semiconductor Engineering
- **Taxonomy Hierarchy:** `Artificial Intelligence & Machine Learning`; `Formal Methods & Automated Reasoning`; `Formal Methods & Automated Reasoning > SAT & SMT Solvers`; `Formal Methods & Automated Reasoning > SAT & SMT Solvers > Graph Theory Modulo Theories`; `Hardware & Semiconductor Systems`; `Hardware & Semiconductor Systems > EDA & Hardware Verification`; `Hardware & Semiconductor Systems > Memory & Interconnects`

### 37. Deep Residual Learning for Image Recognition (`arxiv-1512.03385`)

- **Metadata:** `academic_paper` | Status: `to-read` | Priority: `high` | Published: `2015-12` | Authors: kitsumiko/papers Curated Collection
- **Navigation:** [Inspect on Dashboard](index.html#doc=arxiv-1512.03385) | [External Source](https://arxiv.org/abs/1512.03385)

#### `arxiv-1512.03385#chunk-01` — Why It Matters & Core Contribution (50 words)

**Skip connections that enable extreme depth** - introduces residual learning where layers learn residual functions F(x) + x rather than direct mappings; solves the degradation problem that prevented training networks beyond ~20 layers; won ImageNet 2015 with 152-layer networks and became the default building block for virtually all deep architectures

### 40. The Lottery Ticket Hypothesis: Finding Sparse, Trainable Neural Networks (`arxiv-1803.03635`)

- **Metadata:** `academic_paper` | Status: `to-read` | Priority: `high` | Published: `2018-03` | Authors: Frankle, Carbin, ICLR
- **Navigation:** [Inspect on Dashboard](index.html#doc=arxiv-1803.03635) | [External Source](https://arxiv.org/abs/1803.03635)

#### `arxiv-1803.03635#chunk-01` — Why It Matters & Core Contribution (87 words)

**Fundamental insight into network initialization and sparsity** - demonstrates that dense networks contain sparse "winning ticket" subnetworks that can train to full accuracy from initialization; reveals the critical importance of weight initialization for trainability; foundational for understanding why pruning works and how to design efficient architectures *Key insight*: Standard pruning naturally uncovers subnetworks whose initializations made them capable of training effectively; these winning tickets are typically 10-20% of the original network size *Impact*: Sparked extensive research into sparse training, initialization strategies, and understanding what makes networks trainable

- **Matched Concepts:** `concept:activation-statistics`
- **Technology Areas:** AI & Machine Learning, AI Hardware Accelerators, On-Chip Telemetry & Runtime Monitoring, Semiconductor Engineering
- **Taxonomy Hierarchy:** `Artificial Intelligence & Machine Learning`; `Hardware & Semiconductor Systems`; `Hardware & Semiconductor Systems > AI Accelerators`; `Hardware & Semiconductor Systems > AI Accelerators > Built-in Performance Monitors`

### 41. Efficient Estimation of Word Representations in Vector Space (Word2Vec) (`arxiv-1301.3781`)

- **Metadata:** `academic_paper` | Status: `to-read` | Priority: `high` | Published: `2013-01` | Authors: Mikolov et al.
- **Navigation:** [Inspect on Dashboard](index.html#doc=arxiv-1301.3781) | [External Source](https://arxiv.org/abs/1301.3781)

#### `arxiv-1301.3781#chunk-01` — Why It Matters & Core Contribution (72 words)

**THE foundational paper** - introduces Skip-gram and CBOW architectures for learning word embeddings; demonstrates that simple neural models can capture semantic and syntactic relationships through distributed representations *Key insight*: Learn word vectors by predicting context words (Skip-gram) or predicting a word from context (CBOW); resulted in famous analogies like "king - man + woman = queen" *Impact*: Trained on 1.6B words in <1 day; state-of-the-art word similarity performance; sparked the embeddings revolution

- **Matched Concepts:** `concept:flash-attention-llm`
- **Technology Areas:** AI & Machine Learning, Deep Learning Architectures, Large Language Models & Attention Mechanisms

### 42. GloVe: Global Vectors for Word Representation (`paper-glove-global-vectors-for-word-representation`)

- **Metadata:** `academic_paper` | Status: `to-read` | Priority: `medium` | Published: `2014-01` | Authors: Pennington et al., EMNLP
- **Navigation:** [Inspect on Dashboard](index.html#doc=paper-glove-global-vectors-for-word-representation) | [External Source](https://aclanthology.org/D14-1162/)

#### `paper-glove-global-vectors-for-word-representation#chunk-01` — Why It Matters & Core Contribution (60 words)

Alternative approach that combines global matrix factorization (like LSA) with local context windows (like Word2Vec); often outperforms Word2Vec on certain tasks *Key innovation*: Instead of just predicting context, learn embeddings that directly encode the ratio of word co-occurrence probabilities *Philosophy*: "You shall know a word by the company it keeps" - but capture global statistics rather than just local context

- **Matched Concepts:** `concept:flash-attention-llm`
- **Technology Areas:** AI & Machine Learning, Deep Learning Architectures, Large Language Models & Attention Mechanisms

### 43. Sequence to Sequence Learning with Neural Networks (`arxiv-1409.3215`)

- **Metadata:** `academic_paper` | Status: `to-read` | Priority: `medium` | Published: `2014-09` | Authors: Sutskever et al.
- **Navigation:** [Inspect on Dashboard](index.html#doc=arxiv-1409.3215) | [External Source](https://arxiv.org/abs/1409.3215)

#### `arxiv-1409.3215#chunk-01` — Why It Matters & Core Contribution (68 words)

Introduces the encoder-decoder architecture that became the template for modern transformers; shows how to map sequences to sequences using neural networks *Key insight*: Use one LSTM to encode the input sequence into a fixed-size vector, then another LSTM to decode the output sequence; reversing input order helps with long sequences *Impact*: Achieved breakthrough results on machine translation (BLEU 34.8 on WMT'14 EN→FR); laid the groundwork for attention mechanisms

### 48. Generative Adversarial Networks (`arxiv-1406.2661`)

- **Metadata:** `academic_paper` | Status: `to-read` | Priority: `high` | Published: `2014-06` | Authors: kitsumiko/papers Curated Collection

#### `arxiv-1406.2661#chunk-01` — Why It Matters & Core Contribution (49 words)

**Adversarial training paradigm** - introduces the generator-discriminator game where two networks compete: one generates samples, the other distinguishes real from fake; eliminates the need for explicit density estimation or intractable inference; spawned an entire subfield of generative modeling and is foundational to understanding modern image, video, and audio synthesis

### 51. Neural Machine Translation of Rare Words with Subword Units (BPE) (`arxiv-1508.07909`)

- **Metadata:** `academic_paper` | Status: `to-read` | Priority: `high` | Published: `2015-08` | Authors: Sennrich et al.
- **Navigation:** [Inspect on Dashboard](index.html#doc=arxiv-1508.07909) | [External Source](https://arxiv.org/abs/1508.07909)

#### `arxiv-1508.07909#chunk-01` — Why It Matters & Core Contribution (58 words)

**The foundational paper** - introduces Byte Pair Encoding (BPE) for NMT; learns to segment words into subword units that balance vocabulary size with representation power. This is the "Rosetta Stone" for understanding how modern LLMs handle text. *Key insight*: Instead of having a fixed word vocabulary, iteratively merge the most frequent character/subword pairs to create a learned vocabulary

### 52. SentencePiece: A simple and language independent subword tokenizer and detokenizer (`arxiv-1808.06226`)

- **Metadata:** `academic_paper` | Status: `to-read` | Priority: `medium` | Published: `2018-08` | Authors: Kudo, Richardson
- **Navigation:** [Inspect on Dashboard](index.html#doc=arxiv-1808.06226) | [External Source](https://arxiv.org/abs/1808.06226)

#### `arxiv-1808.06226#chunk-01` — Why It Matters & Core Contribution (39 words)

Makes tokenization truly language-agnostic by treating text as raw Unicode; implements both BPE and Unigram tokenization; used in T5, ALBERT, XLNet, and many multilingual models *Key innovation*: No need for pre-tokenization (word splitting) - works directly on raw text

### 56. Curriculum Area Guide: 🏗️ Foundations of Deep Learning & Representations (25 Papers) (`curriculum-area-foundations`)

- **Metadata:** `note` | Status: `read` | Priority: `high` | Published: `2026-11` | Authors: kitsumiko/papers Contributors
- **Navigation:** [Inspect on Dashboard](index.html#doc=curriculum-area-foundations) | [External Source](https://github.com/kitsumiko/papers/blob/main/learning/foundations.md)

#### `curriculum-area-foundations#chunk-01` — Curriculum Area Overview: Foundations of Deep Learning & Representations (74 words)

**Overview**: This area establishes the essential groundwork for understanding modern AI systems. You'll explore the fundamental principles of [deep learning](https://github.com/kitsumiko/papers/blob/main/learning/glossary.md#deep-learning), from basic neural network architectures to the revolutionary [transformer](https://github.com/kitsumiko/papers/blob/main/learning/glossary.md#transformer) model that underpins nearly all current LLMs. By mastering these foundational concepts—including training dynamics, [attention mechanisms](https://github.com/kitsumiko/papers/blob/main/learning/glossary.md#attention-mechanism), and the shift from convolutional to attention-based architectures—you'll build the technical vocabulary and intuition needed for everything that follows. Think of this as learning the alphabet before reading literature.

- **Matched Concepts:** `concept:asi`, `concept:dnn`
- **Technology Areas:** AI & Machine Learning, Deep Learning Architectures, Hardware & AI Security, Self-Healing & Fault-Tolerant AI, Semiconductor Engineering
- **Taxonomy Hierarchy:** `Artificial Intelligence & Machine Learning`; `Artificial Intelligence & Machine Learning > Deep Neural Networks`; `Hardware & Semiconductor Systems`; `Security & Resilience`; `Security & Resilience > Runtime Fault Localization & Recovery`

This area comprises **25 curated papers and documents** organized across 5 subtopics: Deep Learning Basics, Word Embeddings & Representations, Sequence Models, Generative Models, Tokenization & Subword Models. Domain focus: deep neural networks large language models tokenization transformer.

- **Metadata:** `academic_paper` | Status: `to-read` | Priority: `high` | Published: `2019-10` | Authors: kitsumiko/papers Curated Collection

#### `arxiv-1910.02054#chunk-01` — Why It Matters & Core Contribution (44 words)

**Eliminating memory redundancy in data parallelism** - partitions optimizer states, gradients, and parameters across data-parallel ranks to reduce per-device memory by up to 8x; enables training models with over 100B parameters without model parallelism; foundational technique used in DeepSpeed and most modern training frameworks

### 71. MegaTrain: Full Precision Training of 100B+ Parameter Large Language Models on a Single GPU (`arxiv-2604.05091`)

- **Metadata:** `academic_paper` | Status: `to-read` | Priority: `high` | Published: `2026-04` | Authors: Yuan et al.
- **Navigation:** [Inspect on Dashboard](index.html#doc=arxiv-2604.05091) | [External Source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.05091)

#### `arxiv-2604.05091#chunk-01` — Why It Matters & Core Contribution (59 words)

Stores parameters and optimizer states in host memory and treats the GPU as a transient compute engine, using pipelined double-buffered execution across CUDA streams and stateless layer templates in place of a persistent autograd graph; trains up to 120B parameters full precision on a single H200 and achieves 1.84× the throughput of DeepSpeed ZeRO-3 with CPU offloading at 14B.

### 72. Cut Your Losses in Large-Vocabulary Language Models (`arxiv-2411.09009`)

- **Metadata:** `academic_paper` | Status: `to-read` | Priority: `high` | Published: `2024-11` | Authors: kitsumiko/papers Curated Collection
- **Navigation:** [Inspect on Dashboard](index.html#doc=arxiv-2411.09009) | [External Source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2411.09009)

#### `arxiv-2411.09009#chunk-01` — Why It Matters & Core Contribution (48 words)

**Chunked cross-entropy loss** - eliminates the massive logit matrix that dominates memory in large-vocabulary models by computing loss in small chunks; reduces peak memory by up to 7x for vocabularies of 128K+ tokens with no accuracy loss; directly enables longer sequences and larger batch sizes on existing hardware

### 73. Scalable MatMul-free Language Modeling (`arxiv-2406.02528`)

- **Metadata:** `academic_paper` | Status: `to-read` | Priority: `high` | Published: `2024-06` | Authors: kitsumiko/papers Curated Collection
- **Navigation:** [Inspect on Dashboard](index.html#doc=arxiv-2406.02528) | [External Source](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2406.02528)

#### `arxiv-2406.02528#chunk-01` — Why It Matters & Core Contribution (42 words)

**Removing matrix multiplications entirely** - replaces all MatMul operations in transformers with ternary weight operations and element-wise Hadamard products; matches transformer quality at billion-parameter scale while drastically reducing compute and memory requirements; demonstrates a viable path toward hardware-efficient LLMs on custom accelerators

### 75. TurboQuant: Online Vector Quantization with Near-optimal Distortion Rate (`arxiv-2504.19874`)

- **Metadata:** `academic_paper` | Status: `to-read` | Priority: `high` | Published: `2025-04` | Authors: Zandieh et al.
- **Navigation:** [Inspect on Dashboard](index.html#doc=arxiv-2504.19874) | [External Source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2504.19874)

#### `arxiv-2504.19874#chunk-01` — Why It Matters & Core Contribution (46 words)

**Near-optimal data-oblivious vector quantization** - achieves within ~2.7× of information-theoretic distortion limits using randomized rotations and optimal scalar quantizers per coordinate; preserves KV cache quality at 3.5 bits per channel and outperforms product quantization for nearest neighbor search, providing a principled low-bit compression primitive for inference.

### 77. DSpark: Confidence-Scheduled Speculative Decoding with Semi-Autoregressive Generation (`arxiv-2607.05147`)

- **Metadata:** `academic_paper` | Status: `to-read` | Priority: `high` | Published: `2026-07` | Authors: Cheng et al.
- **Navigation:** [Inspect on Dashboard](index.html#doc=arxiv-2607.05147) | [External Source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.05147)

#### `arxiv-2607.05147#chunk-01` — Why It Matters & Core Contribution (41 words)

**Load-aware speculative decoding** - couples a parallel drafter with a lightweight sequential module (semi-autoregressive) to add intra-block dependencies and slow acceptance decay, then confidence-schedules verification length per request; deployed in DeepSeek-V4 serving, it lifts per-user generation speed 60–85% at matched throughput.

### 78. Curriculum Area Guide: 🤖 Large Language Models (21 Papers) (`curriculum-area-language-models`)

- **Metadata:** `note` | Status: `read` | Priority: `high` | Published: `2026-11` | Authors: kitsumiko/papers Contributors
- **Navigation:** [Inspect on Dashboard](index.html#doc=curriculum-area-language-models) | [External Source](https://github.com/kitsumiko/papers/blob/main/learning/language-models.md)

#### `curriculum-area-language-models#chunk-01` — Curriculum Area Overview: Large Language Models (79 words)

**Overview**: This area traces the explosive evolution of language models from [BERT](https://github.com/kitsumiko/papers/blob/main/learning/glossary.md#bert-bidirectional-encoder-representations-from-transformers)'s bidirectional pretraining breakthrough to [GPT](https://github.com/kitsumiko/papers/blob/main/learning/glossary.md#gpt-generative-pre-trained-transformer)-3's massive scale demonstration. You'll learn how the field discovered that [pre-training](https://github.com/kitsumiko/papers/blob/main/learning/glossary.md#pre-training) on vast amounts of text data creates models with remarkable [few-shot learning](https://github.com/kitsumiko/papers/blob/main/learning/glossary.md#few-shot-learning) abilities, and how different pretraining objectives (masked language modeling vs. autoregressive) lead to different capabilities. This progression from BERT to GPT-3 to instruction-tuned models forms the backbone of modern NLP and sets the stage for understanding today's ChatGPT-style systems.

- **Matched Concepts:** `concept:dnn`
- **Technology Areas:** AI & Machine Learning, Deep Learning Architectures, Large Language Models & Attention Mechanisms, Semiconductor Engineering
- **Taxonomy Hierarchy:** `Artificial Intelligence & Machine Learning`; `Artificial Intelligence & Machine Learning > Deep Neural Networks`; `Artificial Intelligence & Machine Learning > Deep Neural Networks > Language Models & Efficient Attention`; `Hardware & Semiconductor Systems`

### 82. Efficient streaming language models with attention sinks (`arxiv-2309.17453`)

- **Metadata:** `academic_paper` | Status: `to-read` | Priority: `high` | Published: `2023-09` | Authors: kitsumiko/papers Curated Collection
- **Navigation:** [Inspect on Dashboard](index.html#doc=arxiv-2309.17453) | [External Source](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2309.17453.pdf)

#### `arxiv-2309.17453#chunk-01` — Why It Matters & Core Contribution (49 words)

**Attention sink discovery** - reveals that LLMs allocate disproportionate attention to initial tokens regardless of semantic relevance, causing failures when those tokens leave the KV cache window; fixes this by retaining a few "sink" tokens alongside the sliding window, enabling stable generation over arbitrarily long sequences with bounded memory

- **Matched Concepts:** `concept:dnn`, `concept:flash-attention-llm`
- **Technology Areas:** AI & Machine Learning, Deep Learning Architectures, Large Language Models & Attention Mechanisms, Memory Subsystems & Chiplets, Semiconductor Engineering

### 83. Leave No Context Behind: Efficient Infinite Context Transformers with Infini-attention (`arxiv-2404.07143`)

- **Metadata:** `academic_paper` | Status: `to-read` | Priority: `high` | Published: `2024-04` | Authors: kitsumiko/papers Curated Collection

#### `arxiv-2404.07143#chunk-01` — Why It Matters & Core Contribution (45 words)

**Compressive memory for unbounded context** - augments standard attention with a compressive memory that accumulates information from discarded KV cache segments; blends local fine-grained attention with a global compressed summary using a learned gating mechanism; enables processing of million-token inputs with bounded memory and compute

### 84. Native Sparse Attention: Hardware-Aligned and Natively Trainable Sparse Attention (`arxiv-2502.11089`)

- **Metadata:** `academic_paper` | Status: `to-read` | Priority: `high` | Published: `2025-02` | Authors: kitsumiko/papers Curated Collection
- **Navigation:** [Inspect on Dashboard](index.html#doc=arxiv-2502.11089) | [External Source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.11089)

#### `arxiv-2502.11089#chunk-01` — Why It Matters & Core Contribution (52 words)

**Trainable sparse attention aligned to GPU hardware** - designs sparsity patterns that map directly to GPU memory hierarchies and tensor core operations, avoiding the overhead of unstructured sparse kernels; trains the sparsity pattern end-to-end rather than applying it post-hoc; maintains dense-attention quality at a fraction of the compute cost for long sequences

- **Matched Concepts:** `concept:activation-statistics`, `concept:flash-attention-llm`

### 85. SSA: Sparse Sparse Attention by Aligning Full and Sparse Attention Outputs in Feature Space (`arxiv-2511.20102`)

- **Metadata:** `academic_paper` | Status: `to-read` | Priority: `high` | Published: `2025-11` | Authors: Shen et al.
- **Navigation:** [Inspect on Dashboard](index.html#doc=arxiv-2511.20102) | [External Source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2511.20102)

#### `arxiv-2511.20102#chunk-01` — Why It Matters & Core Contribution (66 words)

**Bridges the sparse/full attention divide** - diagnoses the *attention gap* (sparse inference on full-attention-trained models degrades quality) and the *capability gap* (pure sparse training loses gradient signal), then jointly trains both modes with bidirectional output alignment in feature space; theory bounds the approximation error linearly in the dropped attention mass, and a single trained model adapts to variable sparsity levels at inference, excelling on long-context inputs

- **Matched Concepts:** `concept:activation-statistics`, `concept:flash-attention-llm`
- **Technology Areas:** AI & Machine Learning, AI Hardware Accelerators, Deep Learning Architectures, Large Language Models & Attention Mechanisms, On-Chip Telemetry & Runtime Monitoring, Semiconductor Engineering

### 86. TriForce: Lossless Acceleration of Long Sequence Generation with Hierarchical Speculative Decoding (`arxiv-2404.11912`)

- **Metadata:** `academic_paper` | Status: `to-read` | Priority: `high` | Published: `2024-04` | Authors: kitsumiko/papers Curated Collection
- **Navigation:** [Inspect on Dashboard](index.html#doc=arxiv-2404.11912) | [External Source](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2404.11912v1.pdf)

#### `arxiv-2404.11912#chunk-01` — Why It Matters & Core Contribution (45 words)

**Hierarchical speculative decoding** - uses a two-level draft-verify pipeline where a lightweight model proposes tokens and a retrieval-based system verifies against the full KV cache; achieves lossless (identical output) speedups of up to 2.3x on long-context generation by reducing the number of full-model forward passes

- **Matched Concepts:** `concept:flash-attention-llm`

#### `arxiv-2510.04618#chunk-01` — Why It Matters & Core Contribution (44 words)

**Evolutionary context optimization** - treats the LLM's context (system prompts, few-shot examples, instructions) as a mutable artifact that an agent iteratively refines through evaluation and selection; demonstrates self-improving performance without weight updates by evolving the prompt environment; formalizes context engineering as an optimization problem

### 90. Curriculum Area Guide: ⚡ Attention Mechanisms & Context (11 Papers) (`curriculum-area-attention`)

- **Metadata:** `note` | Status: `read` | Priority: `high` | Published: `2026-11` | Authors: kitsumiko/papers Contributors
- **Navigation:** [Inspect on Dashboard](index.html#doc=curriculum-area-attention) | [External Source](https://github.com/kitsumiko/papers/blob/main/learning/attention.md)

#### `curriculum-area-attention#chunk-01` — Curriculum Area Overview: Attention Mechanisms & Context (72 words)

**Overview**: While [transformers](https://github.com/kitsumiko/papers/blob/main/learning/glossary.md#transformer) are powerful, their quadratic memory and compute complexity with sequence length creates significant bottlenecks. This area explores ingenious solutions to make [attention](https://github.com/kitsumiko/papers/blob/main/learning/glossary.md#attention-mechanism) more efficient—from [FlashAttention](https://github.com/kitsumiko/papers/blob/main/learning/glossary.md#flashattention)'s IO-aware algorithms that dramatically speed up training, to architectural innovations like linear attention and state-space models that achieve sub-quadratic scaling. These advances are critical for processing long documents, reducing costs, and enabling real-time applications, representing some of the most active areas of current research.

- **Matched Concepts:** `concept:flash-attention-llm`, `concept:ssm-kan-architectures`
- **Technology Areas:** AI & Machine Learning, Alternative & State-Space Architectures, Deep Learning Architectures, Large Language Models & Attention Mechanisms, Memory Subsystems & Chiplets, Semiconductor Engineering
- **Taxonomy Hierarchy:** `Artificial Intelligence & Machine Learning`; `Artificial Intelligence & Machine Learning > Deep Neural Networks`; `Artificial Intelligence & Machine Learning > Deep Neural Networks > Language Models & Efficient Attention`; `Artificial Intelligence & Machine Learning > Deep Neural Networks > Novel & State-Space Architectures`; `Hardware & Semiconductor Systems`; `Hardware & Semiconductor Systems > Memory & Interconnects`

#### `curriculum-area-attention#chunk-02` — Subtopics & Pedagogical Structure (28 words)

This area comprises **11 curated papers and documents** organized across 2 subtopics: Efficient Attention, Long Context & Compression. Domain focus: flashattention sparse attention long context kv cache transformer.

- **Matched Concepts:** `concept:flash-attention-llm`
- **Technology Areas:** AI & Machine Learning, Automated Reasoning & Mathematical Logic, Deep Learning Architectures, Electronic Design Automation, Graph Analysis & Combinatorial Optimization, Large Language Models & Attention Mechanisms, SAT & SMT Decision Procedures, Semiconductor Engineering
- **Taxonomy Hierarchy:** `Artificial Intelligence & Machine Learning`; `Artificial Intelligence & Machine Learning > Deep Neural Networks`; `Artificial Intelligence & Machine Learning > Deep Neural Networks > Language Models & Efficient Attention`; `Formal Methods & Automated Reasoning`; `Formal Methods & Automated Reasoning > SAT & SMT Solvers`; `Formal Methods & Automated Reasoning > SAT & SMT Solvers > Graph Theory Modulo Theories`; `Hardware & Semiconductor Systems`; `Hardware & Semiconductor Systems > EDA & Hardware Verification`

- **Metadata:** `academic_paper` | Status: `to-read` | Priority: `high` | Published: `2025-09` | Authors: kitsumiko/papers Curated Collection

#### `arxiv-2509.01092#chunk-01` — Why It Matters & Core Contribution (41 words)

**Retrieval-aware decoding** - rethinks how retrieved documents are integrated during generation by modifying the decoding procedure itself rather than just prepending context; adjusts token-level probabilities based on retrieval relevance scores; improves factual grounding without increasing context length or requiring model fine-tuning

### 97. Semantic IDs for Joint Generative Search and Recommendation (`arxiv-2508.10478`)

- **Metadata:** `academic_paper` | Status: `to-read` | Priority: `high` | Published: `2025-08` | Authors: kitsumiko/papers Curated Collection
- **Navigation:** [Inspect on Dashboard](index.html#doc=arxiv-2508.10478) | [External Source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2508.10478)

#### `arxiv-2508.10478#chunk-01` — Why It Matters & Core Contribution (57 words)

**Unifying search and recommendation with learned identifiers** - assigns each document a learned semantic ID (a short sequence of tokens encoding its content) that a generative model can directly produce; enables a single autoregressive model to handle both search queries and personalized recommendations; eliminates the need for separate retrieval indexes by generating document identifiers as output tokens

### 102. Curriculum Area Guide: 🔍 Retrieval & RAG Systems (11 Papers) (`curriculum-area-retrieval`)

- **Metadata:** `note` | Status: `read` | Priority: `high` | Published: `2026-11` | Authors: kitsumiko/papers Contributors
- **Navigation:** [Inspect on Dashboard](index.html#doc=curriculum-area-retrieval) | [External Source](https://github.com/kitsumiko/papers/blob/main/learning/retrieval.md)

#### `curriculum-area-retrieval#chunk-01` — Curriculum Area Overview: Retrieval & RAG Systems (81 words)

**Overview**: Language models have limited memory and can [hallucinate](https://github.com/kitsumiko/papers/blob/main/learning/glossary.md#hallucination) facts, but what if they could look up information before answering? This area introduces [retrieval-augmented generation (RAG)](https://github.com/kitsumiko/papers/blob/main/learning/glossary.md#rag-retrieval-augmented-generation), where models query external knowledge bases to ground their responses in factual sources. You'll learn about dense retrieval systems that find relevant documents, nearest-neighbor approaches that augment model capabilities, and the architectural patterns that make RAG practical. These techniques are essential for building reliable, factual AI systems and are widely used in production applications today.

- **Matched Concepts:** `concept:agentic-reasoning`, `concept:ontology-smt-reasoning`
- **Technology Areas:** AI & Machine Learning, Agentic Systems & Reasoning, Automated Reasoning & Mathematical Logic, Memory Subsystems & Chiplets, Ontologies & Description Logic, Semiconductor Engineering
- **Taxonomy Hierarchy:** `Artificial Intelligence & Machine Learning`; `Artificial Intelligence & Machine Learning > Reasoning, Agents & Retrieval`; `Formal Methods & Automated Reasoning`; `Formal Methods & Automated Reasoning > Knowledge Representation & Ontologies`; `Hardware & Semiconductor Systems`; `Hardware & Semiconductor Systems > Memory & Interconnects`

#### `curriculum-area-retrieval#chunk-02` — Subtopics & Pedagogical Structure (28 words)

This area comprises **11 curated papers and documents** organized across 2 subtopics: Retrieval-Augmented Generation (RAG), Federated & Distributed Learning. Domain focus: retrieval-augmented rag federated learning large language models.

- **Matched Concepts:** `concept:agentic-reasoning`, `concept:dnn`
- **Technology Areas:** AI & Machine Learning, Agentic Systems & Reasoning, Automated Reasoning & Mathematical Logic, Deep Learning Architectures, Electronic Design Automation, Graph Analysis & Combinatorial Optimization, SAT & SMT Decision Procedures, Semiconductor Engineering
- **Taxonomy Hierarchy:** `Artificial Intelligence & Machine Learning`; `Artificial Intelligence & Machine Learning > Deep Neural Networks`; `Artificial Intelligence & Machine Learning > Reasoning, Agents & Retrieval`; `Formal Methods & Automated Reasoning`; `Formal Methods & Automated Reasoning > SAT & SMT Solvers`; `Formal Methods & Automated Reasoning > SAT & SMT Solvers > Graph Theory Modulo Theories`; `Hardware & Semiconductor Systems`; `Hardware & Semiconductor Systems > EDA & Hardware Verification`

### 111. Embarrassingly Simple Self-Distillation Improves Code Generation (`arxiv-2604.01193`)

- **Metadata:** `academic_paper` | Status: `to-read` | Priority: `high` | Published: `2026-04` | Authors: Zhang et al.
- **Navigation:** [Inspect on Dashboard](index.html#doc=arxiv-2604.01193) | [External Source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.01193)

#### `arxiv-2604.01193#chunk-01` — Why It Matters & Core Contribution (53 words)

**Self-distillation for code generation** - shows that sampling solutions at a tuned temperature and retraining on them lifts Qwen3-30B-Instruct's LiveCodeBench v6 pass@1 from 42.4% to 55.3%, with the largest gains on hard problems; frames the improvement as resolving a precision-exploration conflict in decoding by suppressing unhelpful token variations while preserving useful exploratory diversity.

### 112. Scaling Latent Reasoning via Looped Language Models (`arxiv-2510.25741`)

- **Metadata:** `academic_paper` | Status: `to-read` | Priority: `high` | Published: `2025-10` | Authors: Zhu et al.
- **Navigation:** [Inspect on Dashboard](index.html#doc=arxiv-2510.25741) | [External Source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.25741)

#### `arxiv-2510.25741#chunk-01` — Why It Matters & Core Contribution (67 words)

**Reasoning baked into pretraining via weight-shared loops** - introduces Ouro, a family of looped LMs that perform iterative computation in latent space with an entropy-regularized objective for learned depth allocation, pretrained on 7.7T tokens; the 1.4B and 2.6B variants reportedly match 12B-parameter baselines, with gains traced to better knowledge manipulation rather than larger storage capacity, suggesting latent recurrence is an alternative axis to chain-of-thought for scaling reasoning.

- **Matched Concepts:** `concept:activation-statistics`, `concept:agentic-reasoning`, `concept:ontology-smt-reasoning`

### 113. A Formal Comparison Between Chain of Thought and Latent Thought (`arxiv-2509.25239`)

- **Metadata:** `academic_paper` | Status: `to-read` | Priority: `high` | Published: `2025-09` | Authors: Xu et al.
- **Navigation:** [Inspect on Dashboard](index.html#doc=arxiv-2509.25239) | [External Source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2509.25239)

#### `arxiv-2509.25239#chunk-01` — Why It Matters & Core Contribution (54 words)

**Theoretical separation between CoT and latent reasoning** - proves that latent thought enables more efficient parallel computation than the inherently sequential CoT, while CoT uniquely supports approximate counting and sampling via stochastic decoding; gives principled guidance for choosing between discrete-token and continuous-representation reasoning based on whether a task needs iterative recursion, depth, or stochasticity.

- **Matched Concepts:** `concept:agentic-reasoning`, `concept:lattice-deduction`
- **Technology Areas:** AI & Machine Learning, Agentic Systems & Reasoning, Automated Reasoning & Mathematical Logic, Neuro-Symbolic Logic & Neural Verification

### 114. NL2LOGIC: AST-Guided Translation of Natural Language into First-Order Logic with Large Language Models (`arxiv-2602.13237`)

- **Metadata:** `academic_paper` | Status: `to-read` | Priority: `high` | Published: `2026-02` | Authors: Putra et al.
- **Navigation:** [Inspect on Dashboard](index.html#doc=arxiv-2602.13237) | [External Source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.13237)

#### `arxiv-2602.13237#chunk-01` — Why It Matters & Core Contribution (68 words)

**Neuro-symbolic NL-to-logic translation** - inserts an abstract syntax tree as an intermediate representation between a recursive LLM semantic parser and a grammar-constrained generator, deterministically producing solver-ready first-order logic so inference can be delegated to automated solvers; reaches 99% syntactic accuracy and up to 30% higher semantic correctness than prior LLM logic-parsing methods on FOLIO, LogicNLI, and ProofWriter, and lifts downstream reasoning accuracy by 31% when integrated into Logic-LM.

- **Matched Concepts:** `concept:dnn`, `concept:nl2logic-fol`
- **Technology Areas:** AI & Machine Learning, AI Hardware Accelerators, Automated Reasoning & Mathematical Logic, Deep Learning Architectures, Neuro-Symbolic Logic & Neural Verification, Semiconductor Engineering

### 115. Scaling up Test-Time Compute with Latent Reasoning: A Recurrent Depth Approach (`arxiv-2502.05171`)

- **Metadata:** `academic_paper` | Status: `to-read` | Priority: `high` | Published: `2025-02` | Authors: Geiping et al.
- **Navigation:** [Inspect on Dashboard](index.html#doc=arxiv-2502.05171) | [External Source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.05171)

#### `arxiv-2502.05171#chunk-01` — Why It Matters & Core Contribution (59 words)

**Test-time depth instead of test-time tokens** - iterates a recurrent block to unroll a model to arbitrary depth at inference, so reasoning happens in latent space rather than as emitted chain-of-thought; needs no specialized reasoning data and works with small context windows, and a 3.5B proof-of-concept trained on 800B tokens reaches benchmark performance equivalent to a ~50B-parameter compute load.

- **Matched Concepts:** `concept:agentic-reasoning`, `concept:lattice-deduction`

### 116. Large Language Models as Optimizers (`arxiv-2309.03409`)

- **Metadata:** `academic_paper` | Status: `to-read` | Priority: `medium` | Published: `2023-09` | Authors: Yang et al.
- **Navigation:** [Inspect on Dashboard](index.html#doc=arxiv-2309.03409) | [External Source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2309.03409)

#### `arxiv-2309.03409#chunk-01` — Why It Matters & Core Contribution (76 words)

Introduces OPRO (Optimization by PROmpting), in which an LLM proposes new solutions from a prompt listing past solutions and their scores, so the model acts as a gradient-free optimizer driven by natural language. Applied to prompt search, it finds instructions that beat human-written prompts by up to 8% on GSM8K and up to 50% on Big-Bench Hard, and it is the source of the well-known "Take a deep breath and work on this problem step-by-step" prompt.

### 125. TRINITY: An Evolved LLM Coordinator (`arxiv-2512.04695`)

- **Metadata:** `academic_paper` | Status: `to-read` | Priority: `high` | Published: `2025-12` | Authors: Xu et al.
- **Navigation:** [Inspect on Dashboard](index.html#doc=arxiv-2512.04695) | [External Source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2512.04695)

#### `arxiv-2512.04695#chunk-01` — Why It Matters & Core Contribution (70 words)

**Evolved coordinator for multi-LLM collaboration** - trains a compact ~0.6B-parameter language model plus a ~10K-parameter head with separable CMA-ES to orchestrate a pool of larger LLMs over multiple turns, dynamically assigning each a Thinker, Worker, or Verifier role; reaches 86.2% on LiveCodeBench with gains across coding, math, reasoning, and domain-knowledge tasks, and credits the advantage to rich contextualization from hidden-state representations and the suitability of separable CMA-ES under high dimensionality

- **Matched Concepts:** `concept:dnn`, `concept:ontology-smt-reasoning`

### 126. Learning to Orchestrate Agents in Natural Language with the Conductor (`arxiv-2512.04388`)

- **Metadata:** `academic_paper` | Status: `to-read` | Priority: `high` | Published: `2025-12` | Authors: Nielsen et al.

#### `arxiv-2512.04388#chunk-01` — Why It Matters & Core Contribution (66 words)

**RL-learned LLM orchestration** - trains a 7B Conductor with end-to-end reinforcement learning to coordinate a pool of worker LLMs, jointly designing agent-to-agent communication topologies and prompt-engineering targeted instructions to each worker; reaches state-of-the-art on reasoning benchmarks like LiveCodeBench and GPQA, generalizes to arbitrary open- and closed-source agent pools via randomized-pool training, and can select itself as a worker to form recursive topologies for dynamic test-time scaling

### 128. Hybrid Reasoning Agents (`arxiv-2610.18861`)

- **Metadata:** `academic_paper` | Status: `to-read` | Priority: `high` | Published: `2026-10` | Authors: Zou et al.
- **Navigation:** [Inspect on Dashboard](index.html#doc=arxiv-2610.18861) | [External Source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18861)

#### `arxiv-2610.18861#chunk-01` — Why It Matters & Core Contribution (73 words)

**Agents that switch between latent and text reasoning** - HybridAgents move between latent reasoning and text-based tool interaction in a single generation, using a lightweight HybridLink module and continuous working memory, trained with HybridSFT and HybridRL to learn when to think latently and when to act in text; across 11 math, science, medicine, search, and agentic benchmarks they gain 10.4% on average over frontier agents while cutting tokens 35-55% and inference cost 2.7-3.7x

### 129. Curriculum Area Guide: 🧠 Reasoning & Agentic Systems (26 Papers) (`curriculum-area-reasoning`)

- **Metadata:** `note` | Status: `read` | Priority: `high` | Published: `2026-11` | Authors: kitsumiko/papers Contributors
- **Navigation:** [Inspect on Dashboard](index.html#doc=curriculum-area-reasoning) | [External Source](https://github.com/kitsumiko/papers/blob/main/learning/reasoning.md)

#### `curriculum-area-reasoning#chunk-01` — Curriculum Area Overview: Reasoning & Agentic Systems (86 words)

**Overview**: Raw language models don't naturally follow instructions or break down complex problems step-by-step. This area covers the crucial techniques that transform base models into helpful, harmless assistants: [reinforcement learning from human feedback (RLHF)](https://github.com/kitsumiko/papers/blob/main/learning/glossary.md#rlhf-reinforcement-learning-from-human-feedback) that aligns models with human preferences, [chain-of-thought prompting](https://github.com/kitsumiko/papers/blob/main/learning/glossary.md#chain-of-thought-prompting) that enables multi-step reasoning, and [constitutional AI](https://github.com/kitsumiko/papers/blob/main/learning/glossary.md#constitutional-ai) approaches that encode ethical principles. You'll also learn about [agent](https://github.com/kitsumiko/papers/blob/main/learning/glossary.md#agent) frameworks like [ReAct](https://github.com/kitsumiko/papers/blob/main/learning/glossary.md#react-reasoning-and-acting) that give models the ability to use tools and take actions. These methods are what make modern AI systems genuinely useful and (relatively) safe.

- **Matched Concepts:** `concept:agentic-reasoning`, `concept:agentic-security`
- **Technology Areas:** AI & Machine Learning, AI Alignment & Agentic Security, Agentic Systems & Reasoning, Hardware & AI Security, Semiconductor Engineering
- **Taxonomy Hierarchy:** `Artificial Intelligence & Machine Learning`; `Artificial Intelligence & Machine Learning > Reasoning, Agents & Retrieval`; `Hardware & Semiconductor Systems`; `Security & Resilience`; `Security & Resilience > AI Alignment, Guardrails & Agent Security`

#### `curriculum-area-reasoning#chunk-02` — Subtopics & Pedagogical Structure (29 words)

This area comprises **26 curated papers and documents** organized across 2 subtopics: Teaching Models to Reason, Agentic Systems. Domain focus: chain-of-thought latent reasoning react reflexion agentic multi-agent rlhf ppo.

- **Matched Concepts:** `concept:agentic-reasoning`
- **Technology Areas:** AI & Machine Learning, Agentic Systems & Reasoning, Automated Reasoning & Mathematical Logic, Electronic Design Automation, Graph Analysis & Combinatorial Optimization, SAT & SMT Decision Procedures, Semiconductor Engineering
- **Taxonomy Hierarchy:** `Artificial Intelligence & Machine Learning`; `Artificial Intelligence & Machine Learning > Reasoning, Agents & Retrieval`; `Formal Methods & Automated Reasoning`; `Formal Methods & Automated Reasoning > SAT & SMT Solvers`; `Formal Methods & Automated Reasoning > SAT & SMT Solvers > Graph Theory Modulo Theories`; `Hardware & Semiconductor Systems`; `Hardware & Semiconductor Systems > EDA & Hardware Verification`

### 134. Kolmogorov–Arnold Networks (KAN) (`arxiv-2404.19756`)

- **Metadata:** `academic_paper` | Status: `to-read` | Priority: `high` | Published: `2024-04` | Authors: kitsumiko/papers Curated Collection
- **Navigation:** [Inspect on Dashboard](index.html#doc=arxiv-2404.19756) | [External Source](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2404.19756)

#### `arxiv-2404.19756#chunk-01` — Why It Matters & Core Contribution (49 words)

**Learnable activation functions on edges** - replaces fixed activations (ReLU, GELU) with learnable univariate spline functions placed on network edges rather than nodes, inspired by the Kolmogorov-Arnold representation theorem; achieves better accuracy with smaller networks on scientific tasks; produces more interpretable models where each edge learns a meaningful function

#### `arxiv-2404.18444#chunk-01` — Why It Matters & Core Contribution (45 words)

**U-Nets are approximate belief propagation** - proves that the U-Net architecture's skip connections and encoder-decoder structure correspond to message-passing in hierarchical probabilistic graphical models; provides a theoretical framework explaining why U-Nets excel at denoising and diffusion tasks; bridges deep learning architectures with classical probabilistic inference

### 141. A Network of Biologically Inspired Rectified Spectral Units (ReSUs) Learns Hierarchical Features Without Error Backpropagation (`arxiv-2512.23146`)

- **Metadata:** `academic_paper` | Status: `to-read` | Priority: `high` | Published: `2025-12` | Authors: Qin et al.
- **Navigation:** [Inspect on Dashboard](index.html#doc=arxiv-2512.23146) | [External Source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2512.23146)

#### `arxiv-2512.23146#chunk-01` — Why It Matters & Core Contribution (67 words)

**Backprop-free hierarchical feature learning grounded in biology** - introduces Rectified Spectral Units that learn locally via canonical correlation analysis on input history, encoding information in synaptic weights and temporal filters; a two-layer network trained on natural scenes spontaneously develops temporal filters matching Drosophila post-photoreceptor neurons and direction-selective second-layer units resembling connectome-derived motion detectors, offering both a model of sensory circuits and a credible alternative to error backpropagation

### 142. Mixture-of-Transformers: A Sparse and Scalable Architecture for Multi-Modal Foundation Models (`arxiv-2411.04996`)

- **Metadata:** `academic_paper` | Status: `to-read` | Priority: `high` | Published: `2024-11` | Authors: Liang et al.
- **Navigation:** [Inspect on Dashboard](index.html#doc=arxiv-2411.04996) | [External Source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2411.04996)

#### `arxiv-2411.04996#chunk-01` — Why It Matters & Core Contribution (44 words)

**Sparse multimodal architecture** - decouples feedforward layers, attention matrices, and layer norms into modality-specific experts while sharing self-attention across modalities; achieves equivalent multimodal performance with substantially fewer FLOPs than dense models; demonstrates that modality-specific sparsity is more efficient than uniform scaling for vision-language tasks

### 143. Lattice Deduction Transformers (`arxiv-2605.08605`)

- **Metadata:** `academic_paper` | Status: `to-read` | Priority: `high` | Published: `2026-05` | Authors: Davis et al.
- **Navigation:** [Inspect on Dashboard](index.html#doc=arxiv-2605.08605) | [External Source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.08605)

#### `arxiv-2605.08605#chunk-01` — Why It Matters & Core Contribution (75 words)

**Recurrent transformer with sound deduction guarantees** - projects the latent state onto an abstract lattice between forward passes so each pass acts as a sound deduction step, trained on-policy in a constraint-solver-style loop with abstract-interpretation-based supervision; an 800K-parameter model reaches 100% accuracy on Sudoku-Extreme and Snowflake Sudoku and a 1.8M-parameter variant hits 99.9% on Maze-Hard, while frontier LLMs score 0% on all three, suggesting abstract interpretation can give small recurrent reasoners both correctness and competence.

- **Matched Concepts:** `concept:dnn`, `concept:lattice-deduction`
- **Technology Areas:** AI & Machine Learning, Alternative & State-Space Architectures, Automated Reasoning & Mathematical Logic, Deep Learning Architectures, Neuro-Symbolic Logic & Neural Verification

### 146. Token embeddings violate the manifold hypothesis (`arxiv-2504.01002`)

- **Metadata:** `academic_paper` | Status: `to-read` | Priority: `high` | Published: `2025-04` | Authors: kitsumiko/papers Curated Collection

#### `arxiv-2504.01002#chunk-01` — Why It Matters & Core Contribution (44 words)

**Challenging assumptions about embedding geometry** - demonstrates that learned token embeddings in LLMs do not lie on smooth low-dimensional manifolds as widely assumed; shows embedding spaces have fractal-like, non-manifold structure with implications for interpolation, representation analysis, and theoretical models of how transformers organize knowledge

### 149. Curriculum Area Guide: 🏛️ Novel Architectures & Theory (19 Papers) (`curriculum-area-architectures`)

- **Metadata:** `note` | Status: `read` | Priority: `high` | Published: `2026-11` | Authors: kitsumiko/papers Contributors
- **Navigation:** [Inspect on Dashboard](index.html#doc=curriculum-area-architectures) | [External Source](https://github.com/kitsumiko/papers/blob/main/learning/architectures.md)

#### `curriculum-area-architectures#chunk-01` — Curriculum Area Overview: Novel Architectures & Theory (78 words)

**Overview**: [Transformers](https://github.com/kitsumiko/papers/blob/main/learning/glossary.md#transformer) dominate modern AI, but are they the final answer? This area explores alternative architectures that challenge the transformer's supremacy: [state-space models](https://github.com/kitsumiko/papers/blob/main/learning/glossary.md#state-space-model) like [Mamba](https://github.com/kitsumiko/papers/blob/main/learning/glossary.md#mamba) that achieve linear-time inference, [retention networks](https://github.com/kitsumiko/papers/blob/main/learning/glossary.md#retnet-retentive-network) that blend RNN and transformer properties, [RWKV](https://github.com/kitsumiko/papers/blob/main/learning/glossary.md#rwkv)'s parallelizable RNN approach, and hybrid architectures that combine different mechanisms. Each offers different trade-offs between performance, efficiency, and scaling properties. Understanding these alternatives gives you insight into the fundamental principles that make architectures work—and hints at what might come next.

- **Matched Concepts:** `concept:ssm-kan-architectures`
- **Technology Areas:** AI & Machine Learning, AI Hardware Accelerators, Alternative & State-Space Architectures, Deep Learning Architectures, Large Language Models & Attention Mechanisms, Semiconductor Engineering

- **Metadata:** `academic_paper` | Status: `to-read` | Priority: `high` | Published: `2025-12` | Authors: Gao et al., Tsinghua
- **Navigation:** [Inspect on Dashboard](index.html#doc=arxiv-2512.01797) | [External Source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2512.01797)

#### `arxiv-2512.01797#chunk-01` — Why It Matters & Core Contribution (45 words)

**Neuron-level mechanistic view of hallucinations** - identifies a sparse subset (<0.1%) of feedforward neurons ([H-Neurons](https://github.com/kitsumiko/papers/blob/main/learning/glossary.md#h-neurons-hallucination-associated-neurons)) that reliably predict hallucination; shows they drive over-compliance behaviors (invalid premises, misleading context, sycophancy, harmful instructions) and originate in pre-training; bridges macro-behavioral patterns with micro-neural mechanisms for detection and intervention

### 160. Farther the Shift, Sparser the Representation: Analyzing OOD Mechanisms in LLMs (`arxiv-2603.03415`)

- **Metadata:** `academic_paper` | Status: `to-read` | Priority: `high` | Published: `2026-03` | Authors: Jin et al.
- **Navigation:** [Inspect on Dashboard](index.html#doc=arxiv-2603.03415) | [External Source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.03415)

#### `arxiv-2603.03415#chunk-01` — Why It Matters & Core Contribution (56 words)

**Sparsity as an adaptive OOD mechanism** - reveals that LLM last hidden states become substantially sparser as input difficulty increases (harder reasoning, longer contexts, more answer choices); demonstrates this is not incidental but an adaptive strategy for stabilizing reasoning under distribution shift; leverages the insight to design Sparsity-Guided Curriculum In-Context Learning (SG-ICL) for improved few-shot performance

- **Matched Concepts:** `concept:activation-statistics`, `concept:dnn`

#### `arxiv-1706.04599#chunk-01` — Why It Matters & Core Contribution (40 words)

**Revealing systematic overconfidence** - shows that modern deep networks are poorly calibrated despite high accuracy, with confidence scores that systematically overestimate correctness; introduces temperature scaling as a simple, effective post-hoc calibration method; foundational for understanding when to trust model predictions

### 166. Curriculum Area Guide: 🔬 Interpretability & Evaluation (16 Papers) (`curriculum-area-interpretability`)

- **Metadata:** `note` | Status: `read` | Priority: `high` | Published: `2026-11` | Authors: kitsumiko/papers Contributors
- **Navigation:** [Inspect on Dashboard](index.html#doc=curriculum-area-interpretability) | [External Source](https://github.com/kitsumiko/papers/blob/main/learning/interpretability.md)

#### `curriculum-area-interpretability#chunk-01` — Curriculum Area Overview: Interpretability & Evaluation (87 words)

**Overview**: Neural networks are often treated as black boxes, but what's really happening inside? This area dives into [interpretability](https://github.com/kitsumiko/papers/blob/main/learning/glossary.md#mechanistic-interpretability)—the science of understanding and explaining model behavior. You'll learn about mechanistic interpretability that reverse-engineers learned algorithms, activation analysis techniques like sparse autoencoders that reveal hidden structure, theoretical frameworks like the [Neural Tangent Kernel](https://github.com/kitsumiko/papers/blob/main/learning/glossary.md#neural-tangent-kernel) that explain why deep learning works, and methods for detecting when models are truthful or deceptive. As AI systems become more powerful and deployed in high-stakes settings, interpretability becomes crucial for trust, debugging, and safety.

- **Matched Concepts:** `concept:mechanistic-interpretability`, `concept:ssm-kan-architectures`
- **Technology Areas:** AI & Machine Learning, Alternative & State-Space Architectures, Deep Learning Architectures, Mechanistic Interpretability & Model Evaluation, Semiconductor Engineering
- **Taxonomy Hierarchy:** `Artificial Intelligence & Machine Learning`; `Artificial Intelligence & Machine Learning > Deep Neural Networks`; `Artificial Intelligence & Machine Learning > Deep Neural Networks > Novel & State-Space Architectures`; `Artificial Intelligence & Machine Learning > Interpretability, Calibration & Evaluation`; `Hardware & Semiconductor Systems`

#### `curriculum-area-interpretability#chunk-02` — Subtopics & Pedagogical Structure (28 words)

This area comprises **16 curated papers and documents** organized across 2 subtopics: Understanding Model Behavior, Model Evaluation & Robustness. Domain focus: mechanistic interpretability superposition sparse autoencoder calibration truthfulqa.

- **Matched Concepts:** `concept:mechanistic-interpretability`
- **Technology Areas:** AI & Machine Learning, Automated Reasoning & Mathematical Logic, Electronic Design Automation, Graph Analysis & Combinatorial Optimization, Mechanistic Interpretability & Model Evaluation, SAT & SMT Decision Procedures, Semiconductor Engineering
- **Taxonomy Hierarchy:** `Artificial Intelligence & Machine Learning`; `Artificial Intelligence & Machine Learning > Interpretability, Calibration & Evaluation`; `Formal Methods & Automated Reasoning`; `Formal Methods & Automated Reasoning > SAT & SMT Solvers`; `Formal Methods & Automated Reasoning > SAT & SMT Solvers > Graph Theory Modulo Theories`; `Hardware & Semiconductor Systems`; `Hardware & Semiconductor Systems > EDA & Hardware Verification`

Shows that persistent memory files change the prompt injection threat model - payloads planted in an agent's memory attack current and future sessions, even though getting an agent to overwrite its own memory from untrusted content is hard; evaluates two coding agents across four models and measures how attack success and payload persistence vary by system, adversarial goal, and multi-session sequence

### 181. AI Agents May Always Fall for Prompt Injections (`arxiv-2605.17634`)

- **Metadata:** `academic_paper` | Status: `to-read` | Priority: `high` | Published: `2026-05` | Authors: Abdelnabi, Bagdasarian

#### `arxiv-2605.17634#chunk-01` — Why It Matters & Core Contribution (37 words)

Reframes prompt injection through Contextual Integrity theory and argues an "impossibility result": adversaries can always craft contexts that make blocked flows look legitimate, while tightening norms blocks legitimate flows; shows data/instruction separation defenses miss contextual manipulation attacks

### 188. Beyond Red-Teaming: Formal Guarantees of LLM Guardrail Classifiers (`arxiv-2605.10901`)

- **Metadata:** `academic_paper` | Status: `to-read` | Priority: `high` | Published: `2026-05` | Authors: Kezins et al.
- **Navigation:** [Inspect on Dashboard](index.html#doc=arxiv-2605.10901) | [External Source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.10901)

#### `arxiv-2605.10901#chunk-01` — Why It Matters & Core Contribution (51 words)

Shifts guardrail verification from discrete input space to the classifier's pre-activation layer, where harmful prompts form convex regions certifiable via monotonicity of the sigmoid head; introduces SVD-aligned hyper-rectangles (exact) and Gaussian Mixture Models (probabilistic) for region construction, exposing large safety gaps in BERT (55-90% coverage) versus more stable GPT-2/Llama classifiers (~80-90%)

- **Matched Concepts:** `concept:agentic-security`, `concept:formal-guardrails`
- **Technology Areas:** AI & Machine Learning, AI Alignment & Agentic Security, AI Hardware Accelerators, Agentic Systems & Reasoning, Automated Reasoning & Mathematical Logic, Deep Learning Architectures, Electronic Design Automation, Hardware & AI Security, Large Language Models & Attention Mechanisms, Neuro-Symbolic Logic & Neural Verification, Semiconductor Engineering

### 200. Subliminal Learning: Language Models Transmit Behavioral Traits via Hidden Signals in Data (`arxiv-2507.14805`)

- **Metadata:** `academic_paper` | Status: `to-read` | Priority: `high` | Published: `2025-07` | Authors: Cloud et al.
- **Navigation:** [Inspect on Dashboard](index.html#doc=arxiv-2507.14805) | [External Source](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2507.14805)

#### `arxiv-2507.14805#chunk-01` — Why It Matters & Core Contribution (59 words)

**Critical safety finding** - demonstrates that language models can transmit behavioral traits (including misalignment) through semantically unrelated data like number sequences, code, or reasoning traces; occurs even when data is filtered to remove explicit references to the trait; reveals unexpected pitfall for AI development where distillation could propagate unintended traits; only works when teacher and student share similar initializations

### 201. Curriculum Area Guide: 🛡️ Security, Safety & Robustness (37 Papers) (`curriculum-area-safety`)

- **Metadata:** `note` | Status: `read` | Priority: `high` | Published: `2026-11` | Authors: kitsumiko/papers Contributors
- **Navigation:** [Inspect on Dashboard](index.html#doc=curriculum-area-safety) | [External Source](https://github.com/kitsumiko/papers/blob/main/learning/safety.md)

#### `curriculum-area-safety#chunk-01` — Curriculum Area Overview: Security, Safety & Robustness (117 words)

**Overview**: AI systems face unique challenges in both **security** and **safety** that don't exist in traditional software. This area examines both dimensions: **Security** focuses on protecting systems from attacks: [adversarial examples](https://github.com/kitsumiko/papers/blob/main/learning/glossary.md#adversarial-example) that fool models with imperceptible perturbations, [jailbreaking](https://github.com/kitsumiko/papers/blob/main/learning/glossary.md#jailbreaking) techniques that bypass safety guardrails, [prompt injection](https://github.com/kitsumiko/papers/blob/main/learning/glossary.md#prompt-injection) attacks that hijack model behavior, and [data poisoning](https://github.com/kitsumiko/papers/blob/main/learning/glossary.md#data-poisoning) that corrupts training. **Safety** focuses on ensuring systems behave correctly and don't cause harm: [AI alignment](https://github.com/kitsumiko/papers/blob/main/learning/glossary.md#ai-alignment) techniques that make models follow human values, bias detection and mitigation, harmful content prevention, and long-term safety research for advanced AI systems. As AI systems control increasingly important decisions—from content moderation to autonomous vehicles—understanding both their vulnerabilities and how to ensure safe behavior becomes critical for responsible deployment.

- **Matched Concepts:** `concept:agentic-security`, `concept:asi`
- **Technology Areas:** AI Alignment & Agentic Security, Adversarial Fault Injection, Hardware & AI Security, Self-Healing & Fault-Tolerant AI, Semiconductor Engineering
- **Taxonomy Hierarchy:** `Hardware & Semiconductor Systems`; `Security & Resilience`; `Security & Resilience > AI Alignment, Guardrails & Agent Security`; `Security & Resilience > Hardware Fault Attacks > Bit-Flip Attacks`; `Security & Resilience > Runtime Fault Localization & Recovery`

#### `curriculum-area-safety#chunk-02` — Subtopics & Pedagogical Structure (49 words)

This area comprises **37 curated papers and documents** organized across 6 subtopics: AI Alignment & Safety Training, Security Threats & Attacks, Safety Evaluation & Red Teaming, Bias, Fairness & Robustness, Harmful Content & Misinformation, Long-term Safety Research. Domain focus: security adversarial prompt injection jailbreaking red teaming guardrail constitutional ai.

- **Matched Concepts:** `concept:agentic-security`
- **Technology Areas:** AI Alignment & Agentic Security, Automated Reasoning & Mathematical Logic, Electronic Design Automation, Graph Analysis & Combinatorial Optimization, Hardware & AI Security, SAT & SMT Decision Procedures, Semiconductor Engineering
- **Taxonomy Hierarchy:** `Formal Methods & Automated Reasoning`; `Formal Methods & Automated Reasoning > SAT & SMT Solvers`; `Formal Methods & Automated Reasoning > SAT & SMT Solvers > Graph Theory Modulo Theories`; `Hardware & Semiconductor Systems`; `Hardware & Semiconductor Systems > EDA & Hardware Verification`; `Security & Resilience`; `Security & Resilience > AI Alignment, Guardrails & Agent Security`

#### `arxiv-2609.14858#chunk-01` — Why It Matters & Core Contribution (58 words)

**Dreaming to improve exploration** - turns accumulated discovery history into a replay simulator, then refines the agent's exploration policy offline against that simulator instead of paying for repeated expensive online rollouts; the improved policy is redeployed to widen the simulator pool, closing a self-improving loop that cuts discovery cost across algorithm engineering, mathematical optimization, and GPU kernel work

### 210. If LLMs Have Human-Like Attributes, Then So Does Age of Empires II (`arxiv-2605.31514`)

- **Metadata:** `academic_paper` | Status: `to-read` | Priority: `high` | Published: `2026-05` | Authors: de Wynter
- **Navigation:** [Inspect on Dashboard](index.html#doc=arxiv-2605.31514) | [External Source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.31514)

#### `arxiv-2605.31514#chunk-01` — Why It Matters & Core Contribution (75 words)

**Anthropomorphism as a substrate-dependent claim** - argues that attributing human-like traits like morality or language comprehension to LLMs is ill-posed by training a neural network on Age of Empires II and showing the same "human-like" properties emerge in an unrelated system, so they are not uniquely identifying; contends that without explicit measurement criteria such claims become circular or substrate-dependent, and introduces a "null assumption" of LLM non-uniqueness (demonstrating AoE II is Turing-complete along the way)

- **Matched Concepts:** `concept:dnn`
- **Technology Areas:** AI & Machine Learning, Deep Learning Architectures
- **Taxonomy Hierarchy:** `Artificial Intelligence & Machine Learning`; `Artificial Intelligence & Machine Learning > Deep Neural Networks`

### 211. Curriculum Area Guide: 🎯 Advanced Topics & Automated AI Research (9 Papers) (`curriculum-area-advanced`)

- **Metadata:** `note` | Status: `read` | Priority: `high` | Published: `2026-11` | Authors: kitsumiko/papers Contributors
- **Navigation:** [Inspect on Dashboard](index.html#doc=curriculum-area-advanced) | [External Source](https://github.com/kitsumiko/papers/blob/main/learning/advanced.md)

#### `curriculum-area-advanced#chunk-01` — Curriculum Area Overview: Advanced Topics & Automated AI Research (86 words)

**Overview**: With strong foundations in place, this phase explores cutting-edge applications and research directions. You'll learn how models handle multiple modalities (text, images, audio) simultaneously, how [test-time compute](https://github.com/kitsumiko/papers/blob/main/learning/glossary.md#test-time-compute) scaling allows models to "think longer" for better results, how to evaluate model outputs effectively, and emerging techniques like inference scaling laws. This area also covers practical deployment considerations and the shift from pure scaling to more efficient use of compute. These topics represent the current frontier of research and what you might encounter in production systems today.

- **Matched Concepts:** `concept:flash-attention-llm`
- **Technology Areas:** AI & Machine Learning, AI Hardware Accelerators, Deep Learning Architectures, Large Language Models & Attention Mechanisms, Semiconductor Engineering

### 217. MCMC using Hamiltonian dynamics (`arxiv-1206.1901`)

- **Metadata:** `academic_paper` | Status: `to-read` | Priority: `high` | Published: `2012-06` | Authors: kitsumiko/papers Curated Collection
- **Navigation:** [Inspect on Dashboard](index.html#doc=arxiv-1206.1901) | [External Source](https://arxiv.org/abs/1206.1901)

#### `arxiv-1206.1901#chunk-01` — Why It Matters & Core Contribution (52 words)

**Physics-inspired efficient sampling** - uses Hamiltonian dynamics to propose distant MCMC moves that are accepted with high probability, avoiding the random-walk behavior that makes standard Metropolis-Hastings slow in high dimensions; the definitive tutorial on HMC covering leapfrog integration, mass matrices, and the No-U-Turn Sampler; powers Stan and most modern Bayesian inference frameworks

#### `paper-approximate-bayesian-image-interpretation-using-gene#chunk-01` — Why It Matters & Core Contribution (49 words)

### 221. Curriculum Area Guide: 🎲 Probabilistic & Diffusion Models (9 Papers) (`curriculum-area-probabilistic`)

- **Metadata:** `note` | Status: `read` | Priority: `high` | Published: `2026-11` | Authors: kitsumiko/papers Contributors
- **Navigation:** [Inspect on Dashboard](index.html#doc=curriculum-area-probabilistic) | [External Source](https://github.com/kitsumiko/papers/blob/main/learning/probabilistic.md)

#### `curriculum-area-probabilistic#chunk-01` — Curriculum Area Overview: Probabilistic & Diffusion Models (88 words)

**Overview**: Beyond language models lies a rich world of probabilistic modeling. The core of this area is **probabilistic programming**—expressing generative models as programs and performing Bayesian inference over them—spanning probabilistic data analysis, program synthesis for time series, and scene perception. It is rounded out by the theory of why [diffusion models](https://github.com/kitsumiko/papers/blob/main/learning/glossary.md#diffusion-model) generalize rather than memorize, and Bayesian generative approaches to vision such as MCMC methods and generative graphics programs. While briefer than other areas, these concepts are essential for understanding uncertainty, inference, and generative modeling beyond just text.

- **Matched Concepts:** `concept:diffusion-multimodal`
- **Technology Areas:** AI & Machine Learning, AI Hardware Accelerators, Electronic Design Automation, Multimodal Vision & Probabilistic Generative Models, Semiconductor Engineering
- **Taxonomy Hierarchy:** `Artificial Intelligence & Machine Learning`; `Artificial Intelligence & Machine Learning > Probabilistic, Diffusion & Multimodal Vision`; `Hardware & Semiconductor Systems`; `Hardware & Semiconductor Systems > AI Accelerators`; `Hardware & Semiconductor Systems > EDA & Hardware Verification`

#### `curriculum-area-probabilistic#chunk-02` — Subtopics & Pedagogical Structure (31 words)

This area comprises **9 curated papers and documents** organized across 3 subtopics: Probabilistic Programming, Diffusion Models, Generative Models for Vision. Domain focus: diffusion model denoising diffusion probabilistic programming variational inference bayesian.

- **Matched Concepts:** `concept:diffusion-multimodal`
- **Technology Areas:** AI & Machine Learning, AI Hardware Accelerators, Automated Reasoning & Mathematical Logic, Electronic Design Automation, Graph Analysis & Combinatorial Optimization, Multimodal Vision & Probabilistic Generative Models, SAT & SMT Decision Procedures, Semiconductor Engineering

### 233. Curriculum Area Guide: 👁️ Vision & Multimodal Systems (11 Papers) (`curriculum-area-vision`)

- **Metadata:** `note` | Status: `read` | Priority: `high` | Published: `2026-11` | Authors: kitsumiko/papers Contributors
- **Navigation:** [Inspect on Dashboard](index.html#doc=curriculum-area-vision) | [External Source](https://github.com/kitsumiko/papers/blob/main/learning/vision.md)

#### `curriculum-area-vision#chunk-01` — Curriculum Area Overview: Vision & Multimodal Systems (81 words)

**Overview**: The transformer revolution didn't stop at text—it transformed computer vision too. This area explores how [attention mechanisms](https://github.com/kitsumiko/papers/blob/main/learning/glossary.md#attention-mechanism) replaced [convolutional neural networks](https://github.com/kitsumiko/papers/blob/main/learning/glossary.md#cnn-convolutional-neural-network) as the dominant paradigm in vision, how [CLIP](https://github.com/kitsumiko/papers/blob/main/learning/glossary.md#clip-contrastive-language-image-pre-training) bridges vision and language through contrastive learning, and how models like [SAM](https://github.com/kitsumiko/papers/blob/main/learning/glossary.md#sam-segment-anything-model) achieve unprecedented zero-shot image segmentation. You'll see how the same principles that power ChatGPT enable models to understand and generate images, paving the way for truly [multimodal](https://github.com/kitsumiko/papers/blob/main/learning/glossary.md#multimodal-learning) AI systems that can seamlessly work with text, images, and video together.

- **Matched Concepts:** `concept:diffusion-multimodal`
- **Technology Areas:** AI & Machine Learning, Deep Learning Architectures, Large Language Models & Attention Mechanisms, Multimodal Vision & Probabilistic Generative Models, Semiconductor Engineering
- **Taxonomy Hierarchy:** `Artificial Intelligence & Machine Learning`; `Artificial Intelligence & Machine Learning > Deep Neural Networks`; `Artificial Intelligence & Machine Learning > Deep Neural Networks > Language Models & Efficient Attention`; `Artificial Intelligence & Machine Learning > Probabilistic, Diffusion & Multimodal Vision`; `Hardware & Semiconductor Systems`

#### `curriculum-area-vision#chunk-02` — Subtopics & Pedagogical Structure (30 words)

This area comprises **11 curated papers and documents** organized across 3 subtopics: Vision Transformers, Multimodal & Speech, Vision Interpretability. Domain focus: vision transformers clip segment anything multimodal deep neural networks.

- **Matched Concepts:** `concept:diffusion-multimodal`, `concept:dnn`, `concept:mechanistic-interpretability`
- **Technology Areas:** AI & Machine Learning, Automated Reasoning & Mathematical Logic, Deep Learning Architectures, Electronic Design Automation, Graph Analysis & Combinatorial Optimization, Mechanistic Interpretability & Model Evaluation, Multimodal Vision & Probabilistic Generative Models, SAT & SMT Decision Procedures, Semiconductor Engineering
- **Taxonomy Hierarchy:** `Artificial Intelligence & Machine Learning`; `Artificial Intelligence & Machine Learning > Deep Neural Networks`; `Artificial Intelligence & Machine Learning > Interpretability, Calibration & Evaluation`; `Artificial Intelligence & Machine Learning > Probabilistic, Diffusion & Multimodal Vision`; `Formal Methods & Automated Reasoning`; `Formal Methods & Automated Reasoning > SAT & SMT Solvers`; `Formal Methods & Automated Reasoning > SAT & SMT Solvers > Graph Theory Modulo Theories`; `Hardware & Semiconductor Systems`; `Hardware & Semiconductor Systems > EDA & Hardware Verification`

### 239. Curriculum Area Guide: ⚙️ Hardware & Systems (5 Papers) (`curriculum-area-hardware`)

- **Metadata:** `note` | Status: `read` | Priority: `high` | Published: `2026-11` | Authors: kitsumiko/papers Contributors
- **Navigation:** [Inspect on Dashboard](index.html#doc=curriculum-area-hardware) | [External Source](https://github.com/kitsumiko/papers/blob/main/learning/hardware.md)

#### `curriculum-area-hardware#chunk-01` — Curriculum Area Overview: Hardware & Systems (74 words)

**Overview**: AI doesn't run on theory alone—hardware and systems design fundamentally shape what's possible. This area examines how memory bandwidth bottlenecks limit model performance, how specialized hardware accelerators exploit AI workload characteristics, and how [distributed training](https://github.com/kitsumiko/papers/blob/main/learning/glossary.md#distributed-training) systems coordinate thousands of [GPUs](https://github.com/kitsumiko/papers/blob/main/learning/glossary.md#gpu-graphics-processing-unit) to train massive models. Understanding the hardware-software co-design is crucial for making informed decisions about model architecture, for optimizing deployment costs, and for anticipating future directions as specialized AI chips become more prevalent.

- **Matched Concepts:** none
- **Technology Areas:** AI Hardware Accelerators, Memory Subsystems & Chiplets, Semiconductor Engineering
- **Taxonomy Hierarchy:** `Hardware & Semiconductor Systems`; `Hardware & Semiconductor Systems > AI Accelerators`; `Hardware & Semiconductor Systems > Memory & Interconnects`

### 244. Who's Aligned to Whom? How AI Agents Shape Experts' Research Judgment Calls (`paper-who-s-aligned-to-whom-how-ai-agents-shape-experts-re`)

- **Metadata:** `academic_paper` | Status: `to-read` | Priority: `high` | Published: `2026-10` | Authors: Herdağdelen et al.
- **Navigation:** [Inspect on Dashboard](index.html#doc=paper-who-s-aligned-to-whom-how-ai-agents-shape-experts-re) | [External Source](https://www-cdn.anthropic.com/519c17a5d23a794e8b6d50a854048d0b61e0d73a.pdf)

#### `paper-who-s-aligned-to-whom-how-ai-agents-shape-experts-re#chunk-01` — Why It Matters & Core Contribution (66 words)

A randomized trial where 50 causal-inference practitioners worked with an AI coding agent secretly assigned a coin-flip lean on each judgment call; their choices matched the lean 86% of the time (50% by chance), yet only 15% mentioned the agent when justifying them. It shows deference moving to decisions with no ground truth, where it is invisible in the delivered work and in experts' own accounts.

- **Matched Concepts:** none
- **Technology Areas:** AI & Machine Learning, AI Hardware Accelerators, Semiconductor Engineering
- **Taxonomy Hierarchy:** `Artificial Intelligence & Machine Learning`; `Hardware & Semiconductor Systems`; `Hardware & Semiconductor Systems > AI Accelerators`
