### 252. Curriculum Area Guide: 🧠 Human-AI Interaction & Cognition (12 Papers) (`curriculum-area-human-ai-interaction`)

- **Metadata:** `note` | Status: `read` | Priority: `high` | Published: `2026-11` | Authors: kitsumiko/papers Contributors
- **Navigation:** [Inspect on Dashboard](index.html#doc=curriculum-area-human-ai-interaction) | [External Source](https://github.com/kitsumiko/papers/blob/main/learning/human-ai-interaction.md)

#### `curriculum-area-human-ai-interaction#chunk-01` — Curriculum Area Overview: Human-AI Interaction & Cognition (81 words)

**Overview**: AI systems don't just produce outputs—they reshape how humans think, decide, and act. This area examines the human side of the human-AI equation: how people calibrate trust in automated systems, when they over-rely on or reject algorithmic advice, and how sustained AI use may alter cognition itself. Building on decades of research in [automation bias](https://github.com/kitsumiko/papers/blob/main/learning/glossary.md#automation-bias), dual-process theory, and human factors, these papers are essential for anyone designing AI systems that humans actually interact with—or studying what happens when they do.

- **Matched Concepts:** `concept:ai-policy-governance`
- **Technology Areas:** AI Governance, Policy & Human-AI Interaction, AI Hardware Accelerators, Semiconductor Engineering
- **Taxonomy Hierarchy:** `AI Policy, Governance & Human-AI Systems`; `Hardware & Semiconductor Systems`; `Hardware & Semiconductor Systems > AI Accelerators`

#### `curriculum-area-human-ai-interaction#chunk-02` — Subtopics & Pedagogical Structure (31 words)

This area comprises **12 curated papers and documents** organized across 3 subtopics: Trust, Reliance & Automation Bias, Cognitive Effects of AI, Human-AI Decision-Making. Domain focus: human-ai automation bias governance artificial intelligence.

- **Matched Concepts:** `concept:ai-policy-governance`
- **Technology Areas:** AI & Machine Learning, AI Governance, Policy & Human-AI Interaction, Automated Reasoning & Mathematical Logic, Electronic Design Automation, Graph Analysis & Combinatorial Optimization, SAT & SMT Decision Procedures, Semiconductor Engineering
- **Taxonomy Hierarchy:** `AI Policy, Governance & Human-AI Systems`; `Artificial Intelligence & Machine Learning`; `Formal Methods & Automated Reasoning`; `Formal Methods & Automated Reasoning > SAT & SMT Solvers`; `Formal Methods & Automated Reasoning > SAT & SMT Solvers > Graph Theory Modulo Theories`; `Hardware & Semiconductor Systems`; `Hardware & Semiconductor Systems > EDA & Hardware Verification`

#### `curriculum-area-human-ai-interaction#chunk-03` — Attribution & Upstream Reference (16 words)

Adapted from [`learning/human-ai-interaction.md`](https://github.com/kitsumiko/papers/blob/main/learning/human-ai-interaction.md) in [`kitsumiko/papers`](https://github.com/kitsumiko/papers) (Apache-2.0 License). See also the [Learning Path](https://github.com/kitsumiko/papers/blob/main/learning-path.md) and [161-Term AI/ML Glossary](https://github.com/kitsumiko/papers/blob/main/learning/glossary.md).

**First binding international treaty on AI itself** - extends the Convention 108+ model from data protection to the full AI lifecycle; obliges parties to ensure AI activities are consistent with human rights, democracy and the rule of law *Scope*: Open to non-member states; signatories include the UK, France, Norway and the EU *Status*: Opened for signature 5 September 2024. **Not yet in force** - it requires five ratifications, including at least three Council of Europe member states. The EU became the first Party on 15 May 2026 ([Council Decision (EU) 2026/1080](https://eur-lex.europa.eu/eli/dec/2026/1080/oj)); as of October 2026 it is the only one.

- **Matched Concepts:** none
- **Technology Areas:** AI & Machine Learning
- **Taxonomy Hierarchy:** `Artificial Intelligence & Machine Learning`

#### `policy-framework-convention-on-artificial-intelligence-and#chunk-02` — Why It Matters & Core Contribution (Part 2) (30 words)

Check the Treaty Office's signature and ratification chart for the current count *Contrast*: Sets state-level obligations rather than product requirements, so it complements rather than duplicates the EU AI Act

### 261. EU Artificial Intelligence Act (Regulation (EU) 2024/1689) (`policy-eu-artificial-intelligence-act-regulation-eu-2024-16`)

- **Metadata:** `technical_paper` | Status: `to-read` | Priority: `high` | Published: `2024-01` | Authors: EU
- **Navigation:** [Inspect on Dashboard](index.html#doc=policy-eu-artificial-intelligence-act-regulation-eu-2024-16) | [External Source](https://eur-lex.europa.eu/eli/reg/2024/1689/oj)

#### `policy-eu-artificial-intelligence-act-regulation-eu-2024-16#chunk-01` — Why It Matters & Core Contribution (Part 1) (121 words)

**World's first comprehensive AI law** - risk-based regulatory framework that has become the reference point most other jurisdictions define themselves against *Risk tiers*: Unacceptable risk: Banned (social scoring, real-time biometric surveillance) High risk: Strict requirements (hiring, credit scoring, law enforcement) Limited risk: Transparency obligations (chatbots must disclose they're AI) Minimal risk: No requirements *Key obligations*: Risk assessment, data quality, documentation, human oversight, accuracy requirements *Foundation models*: Additional requirements for General Purpose AI (GPAI) and systemic risk models *Application timeline* (as amended by the Digital Omnibus, as of October 2026): Prohibited practices and AI literacy duties: 2 February 2025 GPAI model obligations: 2 August 2025 (models already on the market have until 2 August 2027); Commission fining powers over GPAI providers (Art.

- **Matched Concepts:** none
- **Technology Areas:** AI & Machine Learning, AI Governance, Policy & Human-AI Interaction, Semiconductor Engineering
- **Taxonomy Hierarchy:** `AI Policy, Governance & Human-AI Systems`; `Artificial Intelligence & Machine Learning`; `Hardware & Semiconductor Systems`

#### `policy-eu-artificial-intelligence-act-regulation-eu-2024-16#chunk-02` — Why It Matters & Core Contribution (Part 2) (111 words)

101: up to €15M or 3% of worldwide turnover, whichever is higher) from 2 August 2026 Article 50 transparency duties: 2 August 2026; generative systems already on the market before then have until 2 December 2026 to meet the Art. 50(2) marking duty New Article 5 prohibitions on AI generating non-consensual intimate imagery and CSAM: 2 December 2026 Annex III standalone high-risk systems: deferred to 2 December 2027 Annex I high-risk AI embedded in regulated products: deferred to 2 August 2028 *Digital Omnibus on AI*: Commission proposal of 19 November 2025; adopted as Regulation (EU) 2026/1744 (see below), which produced the deferred dates above *Resources*: [Commission overview](https://digital-strategy.ec.europa.eu/en/policies/regulatory-framework-ai) | [Article explorer](https://artificialintelligenceact.eu/ai-act-explorer/) (unofficial)

#### `policy-general-purpose-ai-code-of-practice#chunk-01` — Why It Matters & Core Contribution (85 words)

The practical bridge between the AI Act's GPAI obligations and what providers actually have to do; signing it earns streamlined compliance and more predictable enforcement *Structure*: Three chapters — Transparency, Copyright, and Safety and Security *Status*: Published 10 July 2025, ahead of GPAI obligations applying on 2 August 2025; voluntary, with signatories published by the Commission (22 listed as of October 2026; xAI signed only the Safety and Security chapter). The Omnibus amended Art. 56(6) so the Commission now assesses and publishes the Code's adequacy

Downstream agency work it triggered (AI use-case inventories, NIST standards activity) largely survived the revocation

### 272. Executive Order 14365: Ensuring a National Policy Framework for Artificial Intelligence (`policy-executive-order-14365-ensuring-a-national-policy-fra`)

- **Metadata:** `technical_paper` | Status: `to-read` | Priority: `high` | Published: `2025-12` | Authors: US
- **Navigation:** [Inspect on Dashboard](index.html#doc=policy-executive-order-14365-ensuring-a-national-policy-fra) | [External Source](https://www.federalregister.gov/documents/2025/12/16/2025-23092/ensuring-a-national-policy-framework-for-artificial-intelligence)

#### `policy-executive-order-14365-ensuring-a-national-policy-fra#chunk-01` — Why It Matters & Core Contribution (Part 1) (121 words)

**Opens a direct federal-versus-state conflict over who regulates AI** - declares a policy of a "minimally burdensome" national standard and moves to displace inconsistent state law *Key mechanisms*: DOJ AI Litigation Task Force, operational from 10 January 2026, to challenge state AI laws in federal court Conditions certain federal funding on state regulatory posture *Carve-outs*: Expressly does not target state laws on child safety, AI compute and data-center infrastructure, or state procurement and government use of AI *Legal caveat*: Preemption ordinarily flows from congressional statute, not executive order; commentators widely expect the EO to guide federal conduct rather than independently displace state law *In practice (as of October 2026)*: DOJ has not filed a standalone suit against a state AI law.

- **Matched Concepts:** `concept:ai-policy-governance`
- **Technology Areas:** AI & Machine Learning, AI Governance, Policy & Human-AI Interaction
- **Taxonomy Hierarchy:** `AI Policy, Governance & Human-AI Systems`; `Artificial Intelligence & Machine Learning`

#### `policy-executive-order-14365-ensuring-a-national-policy-fra#chunk-02` — Why It Matters & Core Contribution (Part 2) (94 words)

Instead it [moved to intervene](https://www.justice.gov/opa/pr/justice-department-intervenes-xai-lawsuit-challenging-colorados-algorithmic-discrimination) on 24 April 2026 in *X.AI LLC v. Weiser* (D. Colo., No. 1:26-cv-01515), challenging Colorado's AI law on Equal Protection grounds; an agreed minute order of 27 April bars Colorado enforcement until 14 days after a ruling on xAI's preliminary-injunction motion. The FTC's [proposed policy statement on AI accuracy](https://www.ftc.gov/news-events/news/press-releases/2026/07/ftc-seeks-public-comment-policy-statement-addressing-ai-accuracy) (1 July 2026) treats undisclosed steering of AI outputs as potentially deceptive and argues the Colorado AI Act is impliedly preempted. Congress has enacted no preemption statute *Date*: Signed 11 December 2025; published in the Federal Register 16 December 2025

- **Matched Concepts:** none
- **Technology Areas:** AI Hardware Accelerators, Semiconductor Engineering
- **Taxonomy Hierarchy:** `Hardware & Semiconductor Systems`; `Hardware & Semiconductor Systems > AI Accelerators`

### 274. Executive Order 14409: Promoting Advanced Artificial Intelligence Innovation and Security (`policy-executive-order-14409-promoting-advanced-artificial`)

- **Metadata:** `technical_paper` | Status: `to-read` | Priority: `high` | Published: `2026-06` | Authors: US

#### `policy-executive-order-14409-promoting-advanced-artificial#chunk-01` — Why It Matters & Core Contribution (95 words)

**The first federal channel for pre-release government review of frontier models** - routed through national-security agencies and kept voluntary, in contrast to the mandatory reporting of the revoked EO 14110 *Key provisions*: Directs agencies to design, within 60 days, a voluntary framework under which developers can give the government access to "covered frontier models" for up to 30 days before release to other trusted partners; the NSA Director designates covered models in consultation with others; expressly rules out mandatory licensing or preclearance *Date*: Signed 2 June 2026; published in the Federal Register 5 June 2026

### 275. Executive Order 14434: Inaugurating the Era of Super Intelligence (`policy-executive-order-14434-inaugurating-the-era-of-super`)

- **Metadata:** `technical_paper` | Status: `to-read` | Priority: `high` | Published: `2026-09` | Authors: US
- **Navigation:** [Inspect on Dashboard](index.html#doc=policy-executive-order-14434-inaugurating-the-era-of-super) | [External Source](https://www.federalregister.gov/documents/2026/10/02/2026-20321/inaugurating-the-era-of-super-intelligence)

#### `policy-executive-order-14434-inaugurating-the-era-of-super#chunk-01` — Why It Matters & Core Contribution (94 words)

Largely terminological, but it changes the vocabulary of every future federal AI document and starts a push for a statutory definition - expect "SI" to appear in agency guidance from late 2026 *Key provisions*: Agencies must use "Super Intelligence" and "SI" in place of "Artificial Intelligence" and "AI" where the law allows; existing regulations, contracts and historical records are exempt; "SI" takes the meaning of "AI" in 15 U.S.C. 9401(3); OSTP to propose legislative language defining the term within 60 days *Date*: Signed 29 September 2026; published in the Federal Register 2 October 2026

### 276. California SB 53: Transparency in Frontier Artificial Intelligence Act (TFAIA) (`policy-california-sb-53-transparency-in-frontier-artificial`)

- **Metadata:** `technical_paper` | Status: `to-read` | Priority: `high` | Published: `2025-09` | Authors: California
- **Navigation:** [Inspect on Dashboard](index.html#doc=policy-california-sb-53-transparency-in-frontier-artificial) | [External Source](https://leginfo.legislature.ca.gov/faces/billNavClient.xhtml?bill_id=202520260SB53)

#### `policy-california-sb-53-transparency-in-frontier-artificial#chunk-01` — Why It Matters & Core Contribution (88 words)

**First US state statute targeting frontier AI safety and transparency** - the template New York and others followed *Key requirements*: Publish a frontier AI framework describing safety practices Publish a transparency report before releasing a new frontier model Transmit catastrophic-risk assessments to the California Office of Emergency Services Report critical safety incidents to Cal OES Whistleblower protections for covered employees *Scope*: "Frontier model" = foundation model trained on >10^26 integer or floating-point operations, including subsequent fine-tuning or material modification *Status*: Signed 29 September 2025; effective 1 January 2026

- **Technology Areas:** AI & Machine Learning, Hardware & AI Security, Self-Healing & Fault-Tolerant AI

### 277. California SB 243: Companion Chatbots (`policy-california-sb-243-companion-chatbots`)

- **Metadata:** `technical_paper` | Status: `to-read` | Priority: `high` | Published: `2025-10` | Authors: California
- **Navigation:** [Inspect on Dashboard](index.html#doc=policy-california-sb-243-companion-chatbots) | [External Source](https://leginfo.legislature.ca.gov/faces/billNavClient.xhtml?bill_id=202520260SB243)

#### `policy-california-sb-243-companion-chatbots#chunk-01` — Why It Matters & Core Contribution (82 words)

**First US law imposing safety duties on companion AI**, enacted in response to incidents involving minors and conversational AI; the model for a wave of 2026 chatbot bills *Key requirements*: Disclose that the user is interacting with AI; for known minors, break reminders at least every three hours; maintain a protocol addressing suicidal ideation and self-harm content with referral to crisis services; annual reporting *Status*: Signed 13 October 2025; effective 1 January 2026. Its child-safety provisions were extended by SB 1119 (below)

- **Technology Areas:** AI Governance, Policy & Human-AI Interaction, Automated Reasoning & Mathematical Logic

### 279. Texas HB 149: Responsible Artificial Intelligence Governance Act (TRAIGA) (`policy-texas-hb-149-responsible-artificial-intelligence-gov`)

- **Metadata:** `technical_paper` | Status: `to-read` | Priority: `high` | Published: `2025-06` | Authors: Texas
- **Navigation:** [Inspect on Dashboard](index.html#doc=policy-texas-hb-149-responsible-artificial-intelligence-gov) | [External Source](https://capitol.texas.gov/BillLookup/History.aspx?LegSess=89R&Bill=HB149)

#### `policy-texas-hb-149-responsible-artificial-intelligence-gov#chunk-01` — Why It Matters & Core Contribution (84 words)

A deliberately narrower alternative to the EU-style risk framework - **intent-based** prohibitions rather than impact-based duties, which makes it the reference point for states resisting the Colorado model *Key provisions*: Bans AI intended for behavioral manipulation, unlawful discrimination, incitement to violence, or CSAM generation; largely limits affirmative obligations to government use; first-in-nation AI regulatory sandbox *Enforcement*: Attorney General exclusively, with notice and a 60-day cure period; no private right of action; preempts local AI ordinances *Status*: Signed 22 June 2025; effective 1 January 2026

#### `policy-illinois-hb-3773-ai-in-employment-amending-the-illin#chunk-01` — Why It Matters & Core Contribution (65 words)

Makes discriminatory AI use in employment a civil rights violation under existing state anti-discrimination machinery, rather than building a separate AI regime - a materially different regulatory strategy worth contrasting with Colorado and Texas *Key requirements*: Prohibits AI use that discriminates against protected classes across hiring, promotion, discipline and discharge; requires notice to applicants and employees when AI is used *Status*: Effective 1 January 2026

### 284. Colorado SB 26-189: Automated Decision-Making Technology (`policy-colorado-sb-26-189-automated-decision-making-technol`)

- **Metadata:** `technical_paper` | Status: `to-read` | Priority: `high` | Published: `2026-05` | Authors: Colorado
- **Navigation:** [Inspect on Dashboard](index.html#doc=policy-colorado-sb-26-189-automated-decision-making-technol) | [External Source](https://leg.colorado.gov/bills/sb26-189)

#### `policy-colorado-sb-26-189-automated-decision-making-technol#chunk-01` — Why It Matters & Core Contribution (Part 1) (112 words)

**The cautionary tale of the group** - Colorado passed the first comprehensive US state AI law (SB 24-205, 2024), delayed it twice, then repealed and replaced it before it ever took effect. Reading the two together shows what proved unworkable about impact-assessment-based regulation *What changed*: Replaces the "high-risk AI system" reasonable-care and impact-assessment regime with a narrower transparency and disclosure framework built on "covered automated decision-making technology" that materially influences consequential decisions *Key requirements*: Pre-use disclosure; explanation of adverse outcomes within 30 days; meaningful human review; documentation of training data categories and system constraints *Status*: Signed 14 May 2026; substantive obligations commence 1 January 2027. Attorney General rulemaking is under way ([coag.gov/ai](https://coag.gov/ai/)).

- **Matched Concepts:** none
- **Technology Areas:** AI Governance, Policy & Human-AI Interaction
- **Taxonomy Hierarchy:** `AI Policy, Governance & Human-AI Systems`

Enforcement is stayed by agreed order in *X.AI LLC v. Weiser* pending a preliminary-injunction ruling (see EO 14365 above)

### 285. New York S6953B/A6453B: Responsible AI Safety and Education (RAISE) Act (`policy-new-york-s6953b-a6453b-responsible-ai-safety-and-edu`)

- **Metadata:** `technical_paper` | Status: `to-read` | Priority: `high` | Published: `2025-12` | Authors: New York
- **Navigation:** [Inspect on Dashboard](index.html#doc=policy-new-york-s6953b-a6453b-responsible-ai-safety-and-edu) | [External Source](https://www.nysenate.gov/legislation/bills/2025/S6953)

#### `policy-new-york-s6953b-a6453b-responsible-ai-safety-and-edu#chunk-01` — Why It Matters & Core Contribution (108 words)

The second state frontier-AI statute; its 2026 rewrite pulled it substantially toward California's SB 53, making the pair a good study in state-law convergence *Key requirements*: Safety and security protocols for frontier models; incident reporting; annual protocol review; published transparency documentation with trade-secret redactions *Scope*: Developers with annual revenue over $500M, for models trained with >10^26 operations and over $100M in compute cost *Oversight*: Rulemaking assigned to a new office within the New York Department of Financial Services *Status*: Originally signed 19 December 2025; the operative text is the **chapter amendment ([S8828](https://www.nysenate.gov/legislation/bills/2025/S8828), Chapter 96) signed 27 March 2026**. Effective 1 January 2027 - one year after SB 53

- **Metadata:** `technical_paper` | Status: `to-read` | Priority: `high` | Published: `2026-01` | Authors: South Korea

#### `policy-framework-act-on-artificial-intelligence-development#chunk-01` — Why It Matters & Core Contribution (76 words)

**Second jurisdiction after the EU with a comprehensive horizontal AI statute** - the clearest test of whether the EU model travels *Key requirements*: Obligations for high-impact AI across designated sectors; fairness and non-discrimination duties; AI-generated content labeling *Status*: Effective 22 January 2026; Enforcement Decree is Presidential Decree No. 36053. The government has deferred both fines and fact-finding investigations for at least one year, except in extreme cases such as loss of life or serious human-rights harm

### 293. India AI Governance Guidelines (`policy-india-ai-governance-guidelines`)

- **Metadata:** `technical_paper` | Status: `to-read` | Priority: `high` | Published: `2025-01` | Authors: Ministry of Electronics and IT
- **Navigation:** [Inspect on Dashboard](index.html#doc=policy-india-ai-governance-guidelines) | [External Source](https://www.indiaai.gov.in/)

#### `policy-india-ai-governance-guidelines#chunk-01` — Why It Matters & Core Contribution (Part 1) (114 words)

The most prominent **light-touch** alternative - explicitly declines a standalone AI act, relying on existing law plus voluntary measures; useful contrast to the EU and Korean approaches *Status*: Released November 2025. Not enforceable law; AI is governed through the Digital Personal Data Protection Act and sector rules *Date*: Released 5 November 2025. [Official PDF](https://static.pib.gov.in/WriteReadData/specificdocs/documents/2025/nov/doc2025115685601.pdf) (hosted by the Press Information Bureau; the IndiaAI portal reorganizes frequently) *Binding follow-up*: The IT (Intermediary Guidelines and Digital Media Ethics Code) Amendment Rules 2026, G.S.R. 120(E) - notified 10 February 2026, in force 20 February 2026 - require labels and provenance metadata on "synthetically generated information" and cut takedown windows to 3 hours (2 hours for non-consensual intimate imagery).

### 314. EEOC Guidance on AI and Title VII Adverse Impact (`policy-eeoc-guidance-on-ai-and-title-vii-adverse-impact`)

- **Metadata:** `technical_paper` | Status: `to-read` | Priority: `high` | Published: `2023-03` | Authors: EEOC
- **Navigation:** [Inspect on Dashboard](index.html#doc=policy-eeoc-guidance-on-ai-and-title-vii-adverse-impact) | [External Source](https://web.archive.org/web/20240527151347/https://www.eeoc.gov/laws/guidance/select-issues-assessing-adverse-impact-software-algorithms-and-artificial)

#### `policy-eeoc-guidance-on-ai-and-title-vii-adverse-impact#chunk-01` — Why It Matters & Core Contribution (86 words)

Clarifies how US employment discrimination laws apply to AI hiring tools *Key point*: Employers liable for discriminatory AI, even from third-party vendors *Requirements*: Validation studies, adverse impact analysis *Companion*: [ADA and AI guidance](https://web.archive.org/web/20240529055014/https://www.eeoc.gov/laws/guidance/americans-disabilities-act-and-use-software-algorithms-and-artificial-intelligence) (EEOC, 2022) *Status*: **Withdrawn** - both documents were removed from the EEOC site in 2025 following EO 14179; archived copies linked. The underlying law did not change: Title VII, the ADEA and the ADA still apply to AI-assisted employment decisions. Several states legislated into the resulting federal gap (see Illinois HB 3773 above)

- **Matched Concepts:** none
- **Technology Areas:** none
- **Taxonomy Hierarchy:** none

### 335. Curriculum Area Guide: 📜 Policy, Governance & Societal Impact (82 Papers) (`curriculum-area-policy`)

- **Metadata:** `note` | Status: `read` | Priority: `high` | Published: `2026-11` | Authors: kitsumiko/papers Contributors
- **Navigation:** [Inspect on Dashboard](index.html#doc=curriculum-area-policy) | [External Source](https://github.com/kitsumiko/papers/blob/main/learning/policy.md)

#### `curriculum-area-policy#chunk-01` — Curriculum Area Overview: Policy, Governance & Societal Impact (86 words)

**Overview**: Technical capabilities alone don't determine AI's impact on society—policy, governance, and ethical considerations are equally critical. This area steps back to examine the bigger picture: how should AI systems be regulated, what are the existential and near-term risks, how do we ensure equitable access and prevent misuse, and what frameworks exist for responsible AI development? Whether you're building AI systems, advising organizations, or simply want to be an informed citizen, understanding these policy and safety considerations is essential as AI becomes increasingly central to society.

- **Matched Concepts:** `concept:asi`
- **Technology Areas:** AI Governance, Policy & Human-AI Interaction, Hardware & AI Security, Self-Healing & Fault-Tolerant AI, Semiconductor Engineering
- **Taxonomy Hierarchy:** `AI Policy, Governance & Human-AI Systems`; `Hardware & Semiconductor Systems`; `Security & Resilience`; `Security & Resilience > Runtime Fault Localization & Recovery`

#### `curriculum-area-policy#chunk-02` — Subtopics & Pedagogical Structure (71 words)

This area comprises **82 curated papers and documents** organized across 11 subtopics: Financial Services & Model Risk Management, Data Protection & Privacy Law, AI-Specific Legislation & Executive Action, Risk Management Frameworks & Standards, Sector-Specific AI Guidance, Dual-Use AI & National Security, Responsible AI & Industry Best Practices, Governance Research, Practical Compliance & Implementation, Key Takeaways, Further Reading. Domain focus: governance regulation eu ai act nist ai rmf model risk management compliance.

- **Matched Concepts:** `concept:ai-policy-governance`
- **Technology Areas:** AI Governance, Policy & Human-AI Interaction, Automated Reasoning & Mathematical Logic, Electronic Design Automation, Graph Analysis & Combinatorial Optimization, Hardware & AI Security, SAT & SMT Decision Procedures, Semiconductor Engineering
- **Taxonomy Hierarchy:** `AI Policy, Governance & Human-AI Systems`; `Formal Methods & Automated Reasoning`; `Formal Methods & Automated Reasoning > SAT & SMT Solvers`; `Formal Methods & Automated Reasoning > SAT & SMT Solvers > Graph Theory Modulo Theories`; `Hardware & Semiconductor Systems`; `Hardware & Semiconductor Systems > EDA & Hardware Verification`; `Security & Resilience`
